- **Tests.**
  - Unit: `new_installation()` differs from `default()` only in `open_editors` and `hide_log`; an old RON file without the new fields reads as `default()` with nothing unread; the retired field reads with no warning and no `app.ron.unreadable-*` copy.
  - App: the last line shows when closed; the switch opens and closes and keeps its size.
  - Migrate the log tests (`the_log_s_lines_carry_their_time`, `the_whole_log_is_copied_and_saved`, `the_log_takes_the_keys_only_from_its_own_clicks`).
  - Picture `shell_main` Status Bar against Main-status.png.
- **Manual.** 02 "The log", 13-troubleshooting mentions.

**Done, 2026-11-12.** What T2 had built stayed: the Log switch, the module's name with "(modified)" and its path in the tip (U4), the game folder, the Bottom Dock's Log tab with Copy Log, Save Log and Clear Log on its tab bar (their corner was gone since T2), and the notices, which are still strips drawn between the Top Bar and the docks (unchanged; `updates.rs`'s `the_update_notice_appears_and_is_dismissed` now checks the update notice lies under the Top Bar and over the docks and the editor area). Built: (1) **The log's last line** (the kit's `status::StatusLog`, the board's `.sb-log`): while the Bottom Dock is closed, after a separator beside the Log switch, the last line's message without its time (the board has none), in 11 px `text-3` for a plain line (the board's color for it; the log's own plain color is the body text's), `warn` for a warning and `error` for an error, its first line where it has several; cut with an ellipsis to the room the bar's middle has, never under the right-hand items, its time and first lines in its tip, a button named by its first line (since the review: bounded, and named "Last log line: …"); pressed, it opens the log; not shown while the dock is open, nor with nothing said, nor in less than 48 points (`shell::LAST_LINE_LEAST`), and with nothing to show no room is kept. Neither the board nor SPEC draws a count or a dot of unread warnings on the switch, so there is none. (2) **A running job** (the kit's `StatusJob`, now with a mark of a quarter of its bar going to and fro where the job's progress is not known (`StatusJob::unknown` since the review), `max_width`, `name` and `tooltip`): every job the window runs is `Moonglow::job` (`jobs.rs`: Build Module, Compile All Scripts and Compile Scripts, Compile Models, Verify Module, the Round-Trip Check, What the Module Uses, Attach haks, a hak saved, Extract Files, Export as Files, Import Files, an NWSync publish and upload, a project's and a plugin's commands), one at a time: a second asked for while one runs is refused, the log saying "…: waiting for … to finish", so two never show. It stands before the module's name: its title and its bar, named "Compile All Scripts, 40 of 312" (or "…, under way"), its part done the accessible value, its steps and what it says it is doing in its tip. Its Cancel stays in the job's window (the board draws no stop control in the bar), which dims the window while the job runs, the bar's item with it. Test Module is no job (it starts the game and returns), and an area being read or the look at the module's files keep their own notes. While a job's progress is not known its mark asks for a frame every 50 ms, as `poll_job` already did for the job, and nothing once it ends. (3) **The bar's order of yielding** as the window narrows (corrected by the review below): the last line is cut short down to its least room (48 points); then the game folder goes (SPEC section 12: the folder drops before what is in the middle), and the line widens again; then the line is cut again and goes; then the job's name is cut short (its bar kept); then the note of a look under way and the module's name share what is left as before (`share`), the name last. (4) **`Settings::new_installation()`**: the defaults with `hide_log` set (T10 adds the Open editors switch as its second difference); `Settings::read_eframe` gives it where the settings file does not exist, the desktop app's first start; a file that exists, reads or not, or names no key, gives the defaults (an existing user's: the log open), as does a start with no storage folder. 4.1.0's settings (as RON and as JSON) read as the defaults with nothing unread and no `app.ron.unreadable-*` copy; no field was retired since 4.1.0 (three were added: the docks' sizes), so the "retired field" is tested as a field this version does not have, which reads with no warning. (5) **The Bottom Dock's tab bar in a narrow dock** (taken from T14's list): the Log tab shows its icon alone where its words and the tools do not fit side by side, the tools give way whole from the end (Clear Log first, each still in View's menu), and a dock narrower than the tab's icon (24 points beside both side docks in the least window) draws its bar empty; the tab and the tools never overlap or leave the dock. Also, the carry-over from T6's review: `drawing.rs`'s `a_blueprint_s_appearance_page_keeps_its_view_beside_the_preview` (a creature's editor on its Appearance page showing the model the palette's chosen blueprint has, docked and floating: the tab keeps its view, the Preview says it is shown elsewhere). Public additions: `mg_uikit::status::StatusLog`, `JOB_MARK`, `JOB_MARK_PERIOD`, `JOB_MARK_FRAME`, `StatusJob::{name, tooltip, max_width, width, is_cut, mark_offset}`, `mg_uikit::shell::DockTabs::tabs_width`; `mg_ui::Settings::new_installation`, `Moonglow::bottom_dock_rect`, and `Moonglow::start_job_for_tests` (hidden, for tests and pictures). Tests: `status_bar.rs` (the last line shown and gone, its colors in both themes, cut with its tip, pressed, nothing kept without one; the Bottom Dock's height through the switch, a restart and Reset Layout; a job's item with and without progress, its end, its failure and a second job refused; a project's command (`sleep`) shown as a job and gone when canceled; an idle window's frames; a new installation's window; the bar swept from 420 to 1600 by 8 with a long name, a long last line, a job and a long game folder, nodes and drawn texts inside the bar and apart, the name always there and the order of yielding; the Bottom Dock's tab bar swept from 420 to 1000 beside an area with both side docks), the settings' unit tests, and the kit's `a_job_of_unknown_progress_has_a_moving_mark` and `the_log_s_last_line_is_cut_short_and_pressed`; the three log tests needed no change (they found the tools by name already). Pictures: `screens.rs`'s `shell_status` (the last line, a warning, a job with progress and one without), `shell_status_narrow` (420, 640 and 1000 with long texts) `shell_new_installation` and `shell_bottom_dock_narrow` (the dock open beside an area at 420, 560 and 640; T6's `shell_preview_420` is the picture before, its Log tab over the tools and cut), with `shell_main` and `shell_760`, both themes (`target/ui-mock/impl/m3-t7/`); the kit sheet shows a last line and a job of unknown progress. Seen in the pictures and left: the job's own window draws its progress bar's words ("40 of 312") in the bar's light text over the light track in the light theme, half unreadable (the job window's egui `ProgressBar`, M4's dialogs); in a 420 window the log's lines wrap a character a line in its 24-point dock (T14's squeeze).

**Review, 2026-11-12.** An independent review of T7 (committed as `dddc0a21`) found, and this round fixed: (1) The order the Status Bar yields in was described wrongly here, in the manual, the log, `status_bar`'s comment and the sweep's (and in T7's commit message, which stays as made): the code cuts the last line to its least 48 points, then drops the game folder (the line widens again), then cuts and drops the line, then cuts the job's name, the module's name last, as SPEC's section 12 has the folder go before what is in the middle; every description now says so, and the sweep asserts it (the folder never shown with the line under its least room; a width where the folder has gone and the line has widened). (2) A blank last entry (a project command's empty output line, `"  "`) drew a focusable button with nothing in it and took the line's room: the last line is now the last of the log's last 64 entries (`shell::LAST_LINE_LOOK_BACK`, a fixed walk: the log has no revision to key a cache by) whose first line is not blank; with none, nothing is drawn and no room kept. (3) The whole entry was copied and named each frame (a 4 MB entry gave a 4,000,000-character accessible name): only the first 4 KB of an entry is read for its first line, cut to 300 characters with an ellipsis (`LAST_LINE_CHARS`); the tip (its time, its first 8 lines each cut, "… more in the log") is made only in a frame where the line is hovered or has the focus (`Moonglow::last_line_tips`, hidden, counts it for the test). (4) The Bottom Dock's sweep now asserts each stage by what the tab draws (its words, or its icon alone, the tab named "Log" in both), in order of the dock's width: words with the three tools, the icon with the tools going one by one from the end, the icon alone, nothing under the icon's width; and that a tool not shown is in the View menu. (5) The last line is named "Last log line: …", so a message reading "Log" no longer shares the switch's name. (6) The tests' frame-count waits went (a deadline for the job window's Cancel; `settle`), and the real job's `sleep` is killed when the test ends whatever happens (a guard reads the PID the command wrote and kills it if it is still `sleep`). (7) **Reset Layout gives the shell's own layout** (the coordinator's decision, reversible on the owner's word): the side docks unfolded at their first widths and the Bottom Dock closed, as a new installation has it, its first height kept for when it is opened; it had opened the log (the layout from before the shell). (8) T14's list has the 24-point Bottom Dock of the least window; the update notice is checked against the Left Dock's and the Bottom Dock's tops too; `StatusJob::new(_, None)` keeps its meaning (an empty bar, no frames asked for) and the moving mark is `StatusJob::unknown`, as the kit's items are not changed under their callers; a first start with no game found shows "No Neverwinter Nights installation found…" as the last line (`a_first_start_without_the_game_says_so_in_the_last_line`), as the troubleshooting chapter says. The scratch folder `target/test-output/ui-area-zz-debug` (an `area.mod` of 06:17 that day) is not from T7's runs and was left.

**T8. Quick Open.**

- **Goal.** The Top Bar field "Go to resource or command" (Ctrl+P key cap; at most 440 px) with a popover of results:
  - the module's resources (by ResRef and, for areas, by name), the read-only load-order resources, the commands, plugin entries and project commands;
  - empty field: recent areas and recent modules' areas first.

  Ctrl+Shift+P and Help › Command Palette… open it restricted to commands (U7). The modal `palette_window` goes.
- **Files.** New `quick_open.rs` (an index cached by `ws.revision()` and the load order's generation; `Needle` filter; at most 50 rows with `show_rows`). `commands.rs` (`found` reused; a new `Id::QuickOpen` in the `Id::all` extras, as `FullScreen` is), `keys.rs` (`Cmd::QuickOpen`, Ctrl+P, id `quick-open`), `lib.rs:3032-3034` (`shortcuts` waits while the field has focus).
- **Tests.**
  - Unit: ranking (module before game, name before ResRef); an empty query shows recent areas; the index is rebuilt on revision change only.
  - App: Ctrl+P, type "formosa", Enter opens `m1q3_formosa_9`'s tab; Ctrl+Shift+P finds Build Module and runs it.
  - Migrate `the_command_palette_finds_a_command_and_runs_it` and the 14 palette lines; `the_keys_that_work_anywhere_are_all_commands` holds with the new id.
  - Picture `screens::command_palette` becomes `quick_open`, against Main.png.
- **Manual.** 02 "Keyboard shortcuts" (147) and "Menus"; **17-plugin-api.md:274** ("the Command Palette" becomes "Quick Open"); 14-coming-from-aurora if it names the palette.
- **Risks.**
  - A user who bound Ctrl+P keeps their key (Keymap's conflicts).
  - Index cost on a large load order: build lazily on first focus, off the frame if it exceeds 2 ms (time it).
  - `frame_perf` not needed unless the field draws results at rest.

**Done, 2026-11-12.** Quick Open is `crates/mg-ui/src/quick_open.rs`, the Top Bar's middle (`shell::top_bar_row` hands the kit's slot to `quick_open::middle`; `quick_open::after_bar`, called by `Moonglow::ui` right after the Top Bar, draws the popover and does what was chosen). **The field**: the glass, "Go to resource or command", the key cap of the key actually bound (`Cmd::QuickOpen`, id `quick-open`, Ctrl+P; none where it is unbound; said as the field's description), at most 440 points (300 under W 1300), named "Quick Open". A click, typing or the key opens it (bare focus by Tab does not, so Tab passes through to the build actions); Ctrl+Shift+P and Help › Command Palette… (the same ids) open it with `>`, which restricts it to the commands (SPEC names no other prefix). **What it finds**, one list best first: the module's resources (by ResRef, and an area's or a blueprint's name, as the Module panel's Filter; the objects placed in an area, which the Filter also looks through, and tags are not matched), then the commands (`commands::found(app)`, every row the palette had: the menus' commands, plugin commands and entries, project commands, grayed where they cannot run, a tip saying why: `Id::why_disabled`), then the load order's resources (the game's, haks', override's, the module's own layer left out; they open as the Resources panel opens them: `browser::tab_for`). The rule, in the module's comment and held by unit tests: equal, then prefix, then a word's start (after a character that is no letter or digit), then anywhere, then every word apart, then (commands only) with the menu's name; at one tier a name match before a ResRef match, then the shorter name, the name and the ResRef alphabetically, and of one ResRef the file with an editor first (a script's source before its compiled form). At most 50 rows (`MOST`); a line under them, always in sight, says how many more ("N more: type more to narrow them"), and "Indexing…" while the index is made. Each row: the kind's icon, the name, a dim detail (the ResRef and type of a named resource, a command's menu, a load-order resource's layer, a recent module's folder), a command's key cap. **Empty field**: the module's recent areas (`Settings::recent_areas`), most recent first, still in the module; recent modules' areas are not offered (they cannot be opened without opening their module); with no module open, the recent modules (opened as File › Recent Modules opens them, asking about unsaved work) and the commands that work without one. **Keys**: Down and Up move the choice with wrapping, Page Down and Page Up a page (stopping at the ends), Enter runs it, Tab stays in the field while it is open, Escape closes it and gives the keys back to the widget that had them (recorded in `keys_pressed` before a typed-in field gives them up; a pane's arrow keys are untouched, so the area view keeps its keys), and keeps a dialog under it open (`widgets::keep_escape`). A press outside closes it running nothing; a press on it is neither the area view's nor work in an editor under it (it lies on a Foreground layer); the areas opened lately are named at once where the index has not come to them, and frames are asked for while it indexes, none at rest. While the field has the keys it is a text field as any: a key that types is its (a command given a plain letter waits); a key with Ctrl or a function key runs its command (Ctrl+S saves the module, as in any field) and closes Quick Open. A job closes and grays it. **Ctrl+P and a builder's own key**: `Keymap::give_way` (called by `Keymap::new` and after plugins' and projects' keys are taken in) leaves Quick Open without Ctrl+P where another command that works at the same moment was given it by the user, a plugin's manifest or a project's file, unless the user chose Quick Open's keys too (then both have it, a conflict in Options as usual); the key it gives up is recorded as its own for the while, so the settings never keep it unbound. **Yields** (taken from T14, local to the Top Bar): the field narrows with the room the bar leaves it, is capped at 300 under W 1300, and becomes an icon button (the glass, "Quick Open", its key in its tip) where it would be under 240; then the build actions go, then the icon, then the quick actions. Under the icon (or with no room at all, as at 420), the key and the icon open the field over the bar's middle (300 wide at most, inside the window) with the same popover. **The index**: made the first time Quick Open opens; the module's part again when the workspace's revision, its count of resources or its file changes (`ModuleStamp`), names taken 2 ms a frame (`BUDGET`) where the window works in the background; the load order's part again when `Moonglow::load_order` moves and its layers but the module's (label, fingerprint) changed, made on a thread in the application (it holds the game data only while it reads, about 200 ms). The load order's resources are searched as one text (`Game::find`, with `memchr`'s `memmem`, already in the tree through aho-corasick): on the game with TDoD's 150 haks (382,394 resources, a 1-minute load of 8 on this machine) the index takes about 200 ms and its search at most 1.9 ms ("a", 138,306 found), 0.08 to 0.3 ms for longer queries; a whole keystroke there with Chapter 1 open (the popover's rows made, commands and all, nothing narrowed from before) 2.1 ms for "a" and 0.19 to 0.44 ms for the rest; Chapter 1's module part 0.7 ms, its names 43 ms in all; a made-up module of 50,000 named resources 1.1 to 1.6 ms a keystroke (searched entry by entry), so a first one-letter keystroke on both would be about 3 ms. The load order's part holds about 55 MB for those 382,394 resources once Quick Open has opened (an estimate from the entries' size, not measured). At rest Quick Open draws its field and nothing else. **Gone**: the modal `commands::palette_window`, `commands::Finder` and `Moonglow::command_palette`. **What builders notice**: Escape closes at once (the palette's first Escape cleared what was typed); a key with Ctrl runs its command while typing (the palette held every key back); `>` with nothing after it shows the first 50 commands and how many more (the palette listed every one); a command's detail is its top menu. Tests: `quick_open.rs`'s unit tests (the rule case by case, groups, folding of accents and Cyrillic, spaces, long queries, `>`, 50 and the rest, narrowing, the load order's search against the rule, the builds counted on a revision, a resource put in, a new load order, and a load order changed only in the module's layer), `keys.rs`'s `quick_open_gives_ctrl_p_to_a_command_given_it`, and `tests/app/quick_open.rs` (a resource by name, recent areas, 50 and more, the arrows and Page keys, Ctrl+Shift+P and Help's row, Escape back to a field and to the area view's camera, Escape and a press outside with a Properties window open, a grayed command, the single keys, no module open, the user's Ctrl+P and a rebound cap, a job, the index's builds, Chapter 1's `m1q3_formosa_9` and a read-only game resource with the game installed, the field's stages swept from 1600 to 420 by 8 against the room the bar leaves it, the popover inside windows from 420 to 3440 and 320 tall, the icon at 760, Tab through the field, background indexing, and a press on the popover over the area view); `the_command_palette_finds_a_command_and_runs_it` and the palette lines of `plugins.rs`, `projects.rs`, `resources_panel.rs` and `nui.rs` migrated; the tests that took the window's first text field take the first that is not Quick Open's. Pictures: `screens.rs`'s `quick_open`, `quick_open_no_module` and `quick_open_widths` in place of `command_palette`, with `shell_main`, both themes (`target/ui-mock/impl/m3-t8/`). Differences from the plan: typing "formosa" puts the creature blueprint named Formosa first (an equal name), so Enter opens it, not `m1q3_formosa_9`'s editor; the test types `formosa_9`.

**Review, 2026-11-12.** An independent review of T8 (committed as `6d307f99`) found, and this round fixed: (1) Ctrl+Z in Quick Open undid the module's last change (Undo was left to a text field only once typed in): while the field has the keys, the window's commands are on one side of one rule, `quick_open::runs_from_field`, an exhaustive match over `commands::Id` (a new command must be put on a side): saving, building, testing, panels, views, tools and help run (Ctrl+S, F7, F9, F1...); what changes or closes the module, a resource or a tab waits, its keys left to the field (Undo and Redo, New, Open and Close Module, Import, the wizards, New Script, Close Tab and the tabs' and panes' keys, Reload Resources, Clear Log), as do the plugins' and the project's commands; opened with `>` or nothing, the field's own undo starts there. (2) The load order's part had kept only the copy the game reads, so a game resource one module overrode was missing in the next: it is now made of every layer but the module's (the highest copy of each), a resource the module has too shown once, as the module's (a bit set per load-order row, worked out when the module or the part changes), and kept across a generation whose layers but the module's are the same. (3) A file's name with its extension is found (`hello.nss`, `nss` in it at a word's start), a file-name match ranking after a name's and a ResRef's of its tier, and a last word naming a type narrows (`.utc`, `hello nss`); a unit test holds Quick Open to the Module panel's Filter and the Resources panel's search case by case (it finds all either finds). (4) A build's part is taken whenever it arrives if its layers are those of now; (5) a build gives up at once when the game's data is to be let go of (`release_game_data`), the module closes or the window ends (a flag it looks at every 4,096 resources, joined on drop), begun again on the next open; (6) the load order's part is one text of file names searched with memchr and entries of at most 40 bytes (22.4 MB for 382,394 resources, from about 55 estimated before), let go of when its layers change or the game's data is read again, and not kept without game data; kept while a module stays open, so that Quick Open opens again at once; (7) within a group, resources with a view of their own (a tab of their kind, a 2DA, a text: `has_own_view`, from `Tab::for_resource` and the Resources panel's view) come before those shown only as their bytes, and a module resource with no editor opens read-only rather than not at all; (8) the popover's 8-point margin and the background indexing are asserted (the latter with the build held back by a test's hook, `QuickOpen::hold_builds`); (9) the module's stamp is its revision alone (`Workspace::adopt` advances it; the count of resources guarded no path the window takes); (10) each row's detail is its accessible description; (11) grayed rows' tips say what is missing, and Options › Keyboard gives way on the draft as Apply will, Quick Open's row saying "No key while … has Ctrl+P"; (12) while an input method composes, Enter, the arrows and Escape are its, and a menu opened by its key closes Quick Open (the menus open by Alt and their letters and F10 in the least window). Costs after it (1-minute load 5 to 7): the index in 105 ms (from 196), its search 2.05 ms for "a" (186,270 found, the extensions' letters now counted, from 1.85) and 0.10 to 0.33 ms for the rest, a whole keystroke 2.24 ms for "a" (from 2.24) and 0.16 to 0.43 ms otherwise, a module of 50,000 named resources 0.70 to 1.58 ms (from 1.2 to 1.5; the module's single words are searched in one text now, as the load order's are).

**Merge point M3a, after T8.** Coherent:

- The toolbar is gone and every one of its buttons has a home; Top Bar, Left Dock (Module, Resources), Right Dock (Palette, Preview), Bottom Dock (Log) and Status Bar exist.
- Editors still open as today (floating unless moved).
- The manual is true. Tick the Main Toolbar and Window rows of `ui-redesign-moves.md` that are done (all but Start's compass button, which is M4).
- `frame_perf` against the T0 baseline logs.

##### M3b: Editors in the shell (T9 to T11; merge point after T11)

(7) The boards say nothing of a crowded bar (`.doctabs` cuts what passes its end): built as decision 5 asks. **Built.** Each tab's look is laid out before the dock is drawn (`tabs::prepare`, `Moonglow::doc_tabs`): its kind's icon (`tabs::icon`, an exhaustive match over `Tab`: the resource type's icon as the Resources panel draws it, `browser::kind_icon`, a placed object's by its list, the editor's own for Module Properties (`module`), the manual (`book`), Options (`settings`), Advanced Search, the Placeable Gallery, Tile Properties, a plugin's panel (`plugin`: the plugin API names no icons)), its name (`tabs::name`: the title as before, with no star), and whether its own document has unsaved changes: a script's, the talk table's and the side editors' own state as the star said, and for an area (its ARE, GIT and GIC), Area Properties, a conversation, a blueprint, a raw GFF, a NUI window, Module Properties, the factions and the journal, whether its resources differ from the module as saved (`mg_edit::Workspace::differs_from_saved`, new: a document edited is compared by what it holds with the saved copy, read once and kept until the module is saved, the resource taken out or its tab closed (since the review), so an edit undone is not a change; since the review the comparison waits until the document has rested, see the review below; on the game's largest GIT, Tyrants of the Moonsea's 1.3 MB `zhentkeepwar`, in release, 1.1 ms after a change to its last placeable and under a microsecond after one to its root, where writing it out and reading the saved copy each time took 3.2 ms: `doc_tabs::an_area_s_dot_cost`, by hand). A placed object's Properties show no dot (their document is the area's, whose tab shows it). The title is a galley with the icon's room before it and the dot's and close button's after it (`mg_uikit::dock::doc_tab_title`), so egui_dock makes the tab `doc_tab_width` wide; titles are cached by what they are laid out from (`LookCache`, with the display's scale and `script_layout::fonts_made`), and the words drawn in egui_dock's colors for the tab's state. After the dock is drawn, `dock_look::doc_tab_parts` paints each tab's icon (`text-3`, `text-2` in front), dot and close button (`mg_uikit::dock::doc_tab_marks` over `marks`, whose lines it keeps) and registers the close buttons, on the tab in front of each pane and the tab under the pointer (where no window lies over it), named "Close " and the tab's name, tipped "Close this tab", closing as the menu's Close does (`CloseTabs`, asking where it asks, the tab brought to the front while asked as egui_dock's close did); a middle click closes a tab as before. Each tab is a node of role Tab named by its name, selected where in front, its description "Modified" with the dot; the OS window title keeps its star. The tab menu's Close is Moonglow's own (first of the close rows: egui_dock's went with its close buttons); Eject follows. A crowded bar cuts every title alike to fit (`mg_uikit::dock::doc_tab_cap`), never under `DOC_TAB_LEAST_TITLE` (64 points, about eight letters), the whole name in the tip of a tab cut; past that egui_dock scrolls the bar by the wheel, its own scroll bar off (`doc_tabs_style`: 7.5 points of egui's colors under the bar), and a pane whose tab in front changed scrolls it into sight (`Moonglow::reveal_fronts`); no list of tabs (the boards draw none). Split Editor (`Action::SplitEditor`, `commands::Id::SplitEditor`, View › Split Editor, Ctrl+\\ by default, id `split-editor`; `Moonglow::can_split`, `split_editor`): one group: the tab in front to a new group at its right, half the room; two or more: into the group after it (the one before, for the last); the focus goes with it, so the docks follow (`active_editor`). A group that loses its last tab goes (egui_dock's); `keep_middle` now says what it is for groups: the placeholder only where no tab is left. The tab keys act on the focused group (`tab_pane`: the group with the focus whatever its tabs, a window with the focus where it has two or more; Ctrl and a digit too, `number_tab`, which went by the middle before). F6 keeps the editor area one stop (the focused group's tab in front), and the manual's row stays true. The open-tabs record keeps each group with its place in the form 4.1.0 reads (`split right 500`), and where there are two groups or more the middle's pane also carries the groups' tree (`split`, as a window's), which this version lays out first so three groups come back in order. 4.1.0's reader passes over a middle pane's `split` (its `place_pane` reads `split` for a window alone): from the places alone two groups come back as they were, and three or more as groups split off the middle's sides, no tab lost (read in its code by the review; 4.1.0 itself was not run). The entry count stays 233. **What a builder notices.** Tabs carry their kind's icon and a dot for unsaved changes (an area's too); the close button shows only on the tab in front and under the pointer; the arrow at the left of the main pane's tab bar and the close-all button at its right are gone (Close All is on the tab menu; a pane split above or below can no longer be folded to its tab bar, its seam dragged instead); Split Editor; Ctrl and a digit act in the focused group; a crowded bar cuts titles rather than scrolling at once; a kind kept beside the middle opens into the group there. **Tests.** `doc_tabs.rs` (new, 16 and two by hand: the dot for a script, an area (undone, saved), a 2DA and the talk table with the name unstarred and "Modified" said and the window's star; the icons of an area, a script, a creature and Module Properties by the icon recorder; the close button in front and under the pointer, its room kept, its tip, closing its own tab, a middle click and the menu's Close; Split Editor one group to two and back, absent with one tab, a group emptied, the placeholder; the Right Dock following the focused group (game data); the tab keys per group; two groups kept and read back from their places alone; three groups in order; a tool's group left alone; thirty tabs in 800 points; a title cut with its tip; the bar swept from the 420 point window with both docks open to 1600 with 1, 3 and 12 tabs, every tab as it takes the pointer between the bar's left end and Split Editor's room, the buttons apart; a floating window's tabs; a kind kept beside the middle; a tab that asks brought to the front; by hand, a frame's cost with 32 tabs and the area dot's), `tabs.rs`'s `every_kind_of_tab_has_its_icon`, the kit's four dock tests (the title's room, a crowded bar's cap, the parts painted, the accent line with the focus given), `mg-edit`'s `a_resource_differs_from_saved_until_saved_or_undone`. Left as it was, a difference from the boards for T14 or T15: the editor area lies inside egui's central panel's margin (about 8 points), so its tab bar stands 9 points under the Top Bar and 8 in from the docks, where the boards put it flush (the before pictures the same); the tabs' widths are the boards' to within 5 points (`shell_groups_left` against `Main.png`: 144 against 145 for the area's tab with its dot, 156 against 161 for Faribeth's, the fonts' measures). **Review, 2026-11-12.** An independent review of T9 (its commit `6fdcc8b9`) found, and this round fixed, each with a test seen to fail first: (1) the saved copies `Workspace::differs_from_saved` reads were never let go of: they go now when the module is saved (`save`, `save_as`), when their resource is taken out, and when no tab shows their resource (`Workspace::forget_saved_copies`, called by `tabs::prepare`); `mg-edit`'s `the_saved_copies_go_with_a_save_or_their_resource` ("let go of with the save", "let go of with its resource" when broken). (2) The copies were known by the saved data's address, which a freed resource's data could be given again: each holds its data now (`SavedCopy`, compared by `Arc::ptr_eq`), and so does a tab's `ResourceStamp` (`changed_at` moves with every edit but not with a save, so the addresses alone told a save apart). (3) `a_tab_that_asks_comes_to_the_front_when_closed_from_behind` failed now and then ("the window's work never ended: a table's description's folder being read"): a 2DA editor's look for its description was taken in only while its tab was drawn, so one under way as the tab went behind another (here, the area clicked to the front while the folder's first walk was slow) was never taken in and the window's work never ended; it is taken in each frame for every table now (`table_view::follow_looks`), and `a_table_s_look_ends_with_its_tab_behind_another` holds a look under way (`Moonglow::hold_description_looks`, a test's hook) while the tab goes behind (it waited 30 s and failed with that message when broken). The test passed 20 runs of 20, each after a touch and a rebuild, with the whole suite running beside it in a loop (1-minute load 13 to 18). (4) Tests that could not fail or waited by frames: an area's dot over its objects and their comments (`an_area_s_dot_covers_its_objects_and_their_comments`: an object moved and one added in the GIT, a comment in the GIC, each undone and saved; it failed with the area cut to its ARE); the bar's sweep now holds each button inside the bar (the old `shown.width() <= bar.width()` could not fail); the front tabs' lines are read as drawn (`mg_uikit::front_lines_drawn`, beside the icon recorder) and asserted in `the_docks_follow_the_focused_editor_group` and `a_floating_window_s_tabs_look_as_the_groups`; `thirty_tabs_in_a_narrow_window`'s comment no longer promises a tip check (`a_title_cut_short_shows_whole_in_its_tip` has it); every wait in `doc_tabs.rs` and `screens.rs`'s `shell_doc_tabs` is for what is looked for, with a deadline, or for the window's own work (an area's view asks for frames as it animates, so the pictures wait by what they show: `frames_until`); `editor_groups_are_kept_with_the_module_s_tabs` is `editor_groups_are_kept_and_come_back_from_their_places_alone`, saying what it does (this version's reader on a record without the groups' tree), and the Done paragraph, the manual and the log say what 4.1.0 does with the record as the review read its code. (5) Split Editor's tip is "Move the tab in front to another editor group", true with one group and with two (the manual's key row and the parity line with it; Quick Open shows the command's tip). (6) `split_editor`'s one-group branch gave the new group the focus before `show_tab` did: removed. (7) The dot's comparison ran in the frame of each change (up to 1.1 ms on the largest GIT, and a drag that changes the area changed it every frame): a change shows the dot at once, and the document is compared with its saved copy only once it has rested 200 ms (`tabs::REST`) with no button held (`Watch`, `Workspace::differs_from_saved_at_once` for what is told without comparing: a save, the module's data); the window asks for that frame itself, so an edit undone loses its dot with no input. `the_dot_s_comparison_waits_for_the_document_to_rest` (a hidden counter, `Moonglow::dot_comparisons`: none across a 40-frame drag and a second held still, one after; the repaint asked by `tabs.rs`) failed with the rest at 0 ("no comparison during the drag": 1 against 0) and without the repaint ("a frame asked for"). Measured three times back to back against the parent (`658495a7`, built from `git archive` under `target/`), the 1-minute load 3.6 to 7.7, never under 3 (`target/ui-mock/perf-m3/t9/`): area092's edit frame 7.9, 7.2, 7.8 ms before T9 and 8.1, 7.9, 7.9 ms now (its Undo's 7.7, 6.8, 6.9 and 7.4, 8.7, 8.0): over the 6.94 ms budget before T9 already, at these loads (the reviewer's 8.2 at 4.4 is the area's own frame, the dot not in it: the timed edit is the ARE's `Comments`, compared under a microsecond, and now not in that frame at all); a frame with 32 tabs against 2: 258, 248, 256 against 213, 202, 204 µs before, 277, 278, 279 against 216, 211, 216 µs now; the dot's comparison on `zhentkeepwar` 1.07, 1.09, 1.05 ms after a change to its last placeable (off the edit's frame now). (8) In `shell_tab_dragged` the drop overlay over the area toolbar's "Go to Start Location" is egui_dock's drop target (the same shapes over the same place in the parent's build, `screens.rs`'s new `shell_tab_dragged_alone`); T9 had drawn its lines and top strip in `raised`, which over a toolbar read as a dark box and hid the target's shape: `doc_tabs_style` draws them in `text-2`, ringed in `text-3`, as egui_dock draws them in egui's text color (the kit's style test asks 4.5:1 against `panel`: "Light: drop targets 1.27" when broken). **The owner's rulings of the review:** "decision 8" stands (a tool's group left alone takes the editor area, as any group does; the placeholder shows only once no tab is left); a crowded bar showing tabs that read alike ("at_m1q4…" several times) is accepted for now (T15's notes); the Right Dock going while a script's group has the focus and coming back with the area's follows the rule that the active editor fills the docks, until the script editor has its panel there (M4's notes).

**T10. The Open editors switch and stored places (R1 and R3, ruled).**

- **Goal.**
  - Options › General › Editors: **Open editors: As Tabs | As Floating Windows**, with its note. Under it: Open Properties as tabs of one window, Closing a window cancels its changes, Move windows by dragging anywhere.
  - As Tabs opens every editor in the focused group; areas always as tabs.
  - **"This kind goes to a window" is stored** in As Tabs mode (found-on-the-way 5): `remember_window_places` stops skipping `Place::Window` (lib.rs:2187-2189).
  - Eject, or a drag out, stores it; View › Reset Layout forgets it.
  - Escape on a docked tab with OK and Cancel follows R1; plugin panels (`Tab::PluginPanel`) follow R3.
- **Files.** `settings.rs` (`open_editors: OpenEditors`, default `Floating`, `new_installation()` gives `Tabs`), `options.rs` (General page, Editors section; the draft field), `lib.rs:2123` (`place_for` default by mode), `lib.rs:4270-4319` (`OpenTab`).
- **Settings migration.** An existing file has no field and reads `Floating`; no file reads `Tabs`. Stored places keep their meaning in both modes.
- **Tests.**
  - Unit: defaults and the stored "window" place.
  - App, each in both modes: a script opens as a tab or a window; Eject in Tabs mode makes the next script open as a window; Reset Layout clears it; Properties gather in Floating mode only.
  - Migrate `editors_open_in_windows_over_the_area` (keeps Floating) and `properties_and_conversations_open_as_tabs_of_one_window`, `scripts_open_as_tabs_of_one_window` (Floating).
  - Both modes for the existing families. Because `Settings::default()` stays As Floating Windows, the mode every new installation gets (As Tabs, log closed, the docks following the active tab) would otherwise be covered only by new tests. T10 therefore runs these existing test families in both modes, through a shared helper that builds the app with `Settings::new_installation()`: closing a tab with unsaved changes and its Cancel, Escape (per R1), the open-tabs records written and read back, the tab keys per group (Ctrl+Tab, Ctrl+digit, Ctrl+W), and the stored places of kinds.
  - Picture `options_window` against Options.png's Editors section.
- **Manual.** 02 "Tabs and windows" ("Everything else opens in a window of its own…" rewritten for both modes); 10-options (the new switch, the three settings).
- **Risks.** Escape as Cancel (R1). Without an answer, As Tabs users lose Escape on Properties and Options tabs: say so in the manual either way.
- **Inherits from T9:** the editor groups exist (`Moonglow::editor_groups`, `focused_group`, `middle_pane`, `split_of`, `group_off`): "the focused group" for As Tabs is `focused_group`; a kind kept `split right 500` opens into the group off that side where there is one (`group_off`), so a kind's stored place names a group, not one more split; `remember_window_places` notes a tab moved by Split Editor as any move (a script split off is kept `split right 500`). The tab keys per group are tested in `doc_tabs.rs` in the default mode only: T10's run of the families in both modes takes them in.
- **Inherits from T7:** `Settings::new_installation()` exists (the defaults with `hide_log`), used by `Settings::read_eframe` where no settings file exists; its unit test `a_new_installation_differs_from_the_defaults_only_in_the_log_closed` asserts the one difference and says T10 adds `open_editors`. The shared helper above builds on it; `status_bar.rs`'s `a_new_installation_opens_with_the_log_closed` is the window's test of it.

**Done, 2026-11-13.** **Plan against board** (the boards won where they differ): (1) `Options_crop_ed.png` draws the Editors section as the switch and a note of two sentences only; `Options.png` and `src/Options.html` (and SPEC §13.2) add the third sentence ("A new installation starts with tabs.") and the three settings under a dim "Floating windows" label: built as `Options.png`, the crop taken as older. (2) The plan's "Under it: the three settings" is the board's, with the label saying they are windows' settings; they stay enabled in As Tabs (a kind moved to a window is one there too), as the board draws them with As Tabs chosen. (3) Windows and Tabs keeps only Ctrl+Tab goes to the tab used last, as the board. (4) The board lays the General page out in three columns with page icons, OK and Cancel at the header's right end and a label column (the switch at 125 px); left for M4 (the page keeps its one column; the switch follows its label as the page's other rows do). (5) The board's Status Bar hints for Options omit Esc (`Moves2.html`: "it does nothing docked"); ruling R1 makes Escape Cancel docked, so the hints will show Esc: T12's. (6) The Main board draws a creature's blueprint and a script as tabs of the area's group; the Floating board a script window over the area with Module Properties docked: both as built (`shell_open_editors_tabs`, `_floating`). **Built.** `Settings::open_editors: OpenEditors` (`Tabs` | `Floating`, read by `named!`, so a value this version does not know reads `Floating` with nothing unread), default `Floating`; `Settings::new_installation()` gives `Tabs` (with the log closed: the two differences, `a_new_installation_differs_from_the_defaults_only_in_the_log_closed`). A file without the field reads `Floating`; 4.1.0 reads this version's file (`serde(default)`, no `deny_unknown_fields`; held by the unit test with a struct that lacks the field). `OptionsDraft::open_editors` (kept by `apply` where left as found). Options › General opens on **Editors** (`options::editors`): "Open editors" and `mg_uikit::segmented` (new, add-only: the mock's `.seg`, a radio button per choice to a screen reader, `raised` / `accent-fill`, egui's selectable labels unthemed), the note, "Floating windows" in `text-2`, the three check boxes with their names and tips as before. **Where an editor opens** (`Moonglow::place_for`, now `Option<Place>`; `default_place`): a kept place first, in both modes; else `None`, the focused editor group (`focused_group`), in As Tabs, a window of its own in As Floating Windows. Areas open as tabs in both (unchanged). Two kinds open in a window in both (`Tab::floats`): Tile Properties, which `Moonglow::ui` opens and closes as tiles are chosen (a tab of the area's group would cover the tiles), and the model views (`Model`, `InstanceModel`, `ModelCopy`, `Tile`: a blueprint's Pop Out says "a window of its own", the palette's Preview). The block that moved a tab out of an area's pane now runs only for a kept window or split. Properties, conversations and scripts gather into one window only where they open as windows (`gathers`); in As Tabs they join the focused group. Plugin panels (R3) and Options follow the switch with nothing of their own. **Stored places** (`remember_window_places`): a tab seen in another place than the frame before keeps its kind's place unless that place is its default (As Floating Windows: a window, as before; As Tabs: nothing is the default, so `window` is kept: Eject or a drag out). In As Tabs a docked place is kept only for a tab moved by hand (`Moonglow::moved_by_hand`: the tab egui_dock reports dragged, or Split Editor's), since a place is measured from the middle, which moves as groups are split or emptied: found on the way, splitting the area off a group with a script in it kept "script: split left 500". View › Reset Layout forgets every kind's place and leaves the switch. **Escape (R1)**, in the order it is taken in a frame: `widgets::dialogs_begin` reads it (none while a menu or popup is open); the Top Bar's menus (a submenu's) and Quick Open (`keep_escape`) take it; the Left Dock (the tree's Filter, a search field: `escape_left_search`; the tree's own keys) and the Bottom Dock (the log handing its arrow keys back) consume it; the editor area: each tab's body (`tabs::Viewer::body`) sees it before its widgets, an area view drops what is in hand with the pointer over it, a model window closes with the pointer over it (now a floating one only), the window in front with OK and Cancel takes it as Cancel (`escape_taken`), and a docked tab with OK and Cancel (`Tab::escape_cancels`, the same nine kinds as floating) notes it (`escape_docked_seen`) where it is in front of its group and, where a widget had the keyboard as the last frame ended (`keyboard_had`), the last button press was in it (`last_press`: the field's tab, wherever the pointer is), else the pointer is over it or the last press was in it with the pointer on no other group and no window; after the editor area, the docked tab's is kept for the next frame (`escape_docked`) only where no window took it and the key is still there (nothing drawn in or before the tab took it), and in that frame it is that tab's Cancel (Options closes, Tile Properties asks through `CloseTabs`, the Properties kinds through `escape_cancel`); then the dialogs (`escape_closes`, `escape_here`). Readers of Escape that do not consume it (`area_view` over the view, `palette_view`'s prefab rename, `hak_view`'s and `table_view`'s fields, the NUI designer's drags) are covered by the keyboard rule: typed in, the Escape is their tab's, not the tab under the pointer. Limits and tests: not a tab without OK and Cancel (a script, a docked model view), another group's field typed in (the keyboard's tab takes it, not the one under the pointer), Options' Keyboard page waiting for a key, an open menu, Quick Open, the tree's Filter, the log's keys, a drag held, a window in front, a tab that asks (Tile Properties docked with a change). `egui::Context::read_response` was tried for "the keyboard was in it" and dropped: reading a widget's response at the frame's end changed what the next frame's widgets did (24 tests failed, scripts' Replace among them). **What a builder notices.** As Tabs: every editor a tab of the focused group (a creature's Properties over the area in its group, closed by OK, Cancel, Escape or its close button); Eject or a drag out keeps that kind in windows until Reset Layout. Existing users (As Floating Windows): Options › General's first section is Editors, holding the three window settings that were under Windows and Tabs; **Escape cancels Module Properties**, docked as a module opens, with the pointer over it or after a click in it, as a floating Properties window's Escape always has (what was typed in its fields is set back: click elsewhere to keep it); Escape no longer closes a model's view docked by the user; Escape over a Properties kind docked by the user cancels it. Nothing else. **Tests.** Unit: `a_new_installation_differs_from_the_defaults_only_in_the_log_closed` (two differences), `open_editors_reads_floating_without_the_field_and_each_value_back` (no field in RON and JSON, each value back, an unknown value, no file, read by a struct without the field), `settings_from_4_1_read_with_the_log_open_and_nothing_lost` (the field among those 4.1.0 lacks), `open_editors_is_drafted_and_kept`, `lib.rs`'s `the_window_place_is_kept_where_editors_open_as_tabs` (each mode), the kit's `a_segmented_control_is_named_and_chooses`. App, `tests/app/open_editors.rs` (new, each in both modes where it applies): `editors_open_as_tabs_or_as_windows_as_the_switch_says`, `a_kind_moved_keeps_its_place_until_reset_layout` (Eject by the tab menu), `in_tabs_a_kind_s_place_is_kept_for_its_own_moves_alone`, `in_tabs_a_tab_dragged_to_another_group_keeps_its_kind_s_place` (a pointer drag), `properties_gather_into_one_window_in_floating_mode_only`, `a_plugin_panel_follows_the_switch`, `escape_cancels_a_docked_tab_with_ok_and_cancel`, `escape_on_a_docked_tab_gives_way`, `escape_closes_docked_options_but_not_a_docked_model_view`, `escape_on_docked_tile_properties_with_changes_asks`. The families in both modes through `common::Mode` (`MODES`, `app_in`, `put_in`, `blueprint_harness_in`; `Settings::new_installation()` for As Tabs), each body a function over the mode with two `#[test]`s: closing a tab that asks, with its Cancel added (`a_tab_that_asks_comes_to_the_front_when_closed_from_behind[_as_tabs]`), Escape (`escape_closes_the_window_in_front[_as_tabs]`, `escape_in_a_field_is_the_window_s_cancel_but_in_a_search_field` / `…_tab_s_cancel_…_as_tabs`), the open-tabs record (`editor_groups_are_kept_and_come_back_from_their_places_alone[_as_tabs]`, `three_editor_groups_come_back_in_their_order[_as_tabs]`), the tab keys per group (`the_tab_keys_act_on_the_focused_group[_as_tabs]`), the stored places (`a_tool_window_opens_where_its_kind_was_last_docked` / `a_tool_opens_in_a_window_where_its_kind_was_last_put_as_tabs`, `a_tool_window_reopens_in_its_split_of_the_middle` / `a_tool_reopens_in_its_split_of_the_middle_as_tabs`, `a_kind_kept_beside_the_middle_opens_in_that_group[_as_tabs]`). Migrated, keeping As Floating Windows and saying so: `editors_open_in_windows_over_the_area`, `properties_and_conversations_open_as_tabs_of_one_window`, `scripts_open_as_tabs_of_one_window`. Four description tests in `fields.rs` left the field by Escape, now Module Properties' Cancel: they leave it by a click on the Left Dock (`leave_field`). Two Options tests and `screens.rs`'s `options_window` bring the dropdowns below Editors into sight first. **Guards broken once each** (and put back): As Tabs' default a window (13 tests, the `_as_tabs` families among them); a window not kept in As Tabs (`a_tool_opens_in_a_window_…_as_tabs`, `a_kind_moved_…`: "kept: a window"); the middle's moves kept (`in_tabs_a_kind_s_place_…`: "the middle moved, not the script: [module-properties, script: split left 500]"); a drag not noted (the drag test); Split Editor not noted (three tests); hand moves never forgotten (the unit test); the docked Escape never seen (six tests, the `_as_tabs` Escape families among them: "Floating: closed"); the field's tab not first ("the field's tab's"); the recorder's Escape taken docked ("the recorder's Escape"); a kept place ignored in As Tabs (`a_kind_kept_beside_the_middle_opens_in_that_group_as_tabs` and seven more); the groups' tree not written (both `three_editor_groups_come_back_in_their_order` tests, s02 before s01); the keyboard's case off ("closed by the keyboard's", "then the tab's"); the pointer anywhere counted ("the pointer on another group"); the key not looked at again after the editor area ("the log's Escape"); a window's Escape not taken first ("the window's alone"); a docked model view closing ("a docked model's view stays"); model views not floating ("a model's view, a window"); a new installation floating (two unit tests); docked Tile Properties closing unasked (no "Save changes to Tile Properties?"); the draft dropping the switch; the switch read strictly (an unknown value unread); the segment's chosen fill (the kit test). **Pictures** (`target/ui-mock/impl/m3-t10/`, before, dark, light): `options-general` against `Options.png`'s Editors (the board's words, segmented control, note, sub-label and check boxes; the label column and the columns are M4's; the note wraps at the page's width), `shell_open_editors_tabs` (as Main: the script and Faribeth's Properties tabs of the area's group) and `shell_open_editors_floating` (as Floating: windows over the area), `shell_main` and `shell_floating` unchanged from before but for the log's times. **Inherits to T11 and later:** a window is now also what As Tabs keeps for a kind (`window`), and Tile Properties and the model views stay windows in both modes: T11's window bar row serves both; Escape's order above is where a dialog title row's Close goes; the hints' Esc for a tab with OK and Cancel is T12's (`Tab::escape_cancels` says which); M4 decides whether a placed object's Properties stay a tab over its area in As Tabs (the Main board puts them in the Right Dock).

**Review, 2026-11-13.** An independent review of T10 (its commit `60c4efb7`) found, and this round fixed, each with a test seen to fail first: (1) **Escape and a window in front** (a regression): As Floating Windows, a floating script's window in front, the pointer on a part of the docked Module Properties in sight, Escape canceled Module Properties and left the script, where before T10 Escape was the window's alone. The rule now, one for both modes (still R1): the keyboard decides first. Where the keyboard is: the widget that had the focus as the last frame ended (`focus_was`, its place read at the Escape alone, `Moonglow::keyboard_at`, when egui has taken the focus already, so the read changes nothing of it; a focus moved by Tab counts), else the last press (`last_press`). The keyboard in a floating window: the window's, no docked tab takes it. In a docked tab with OK and Cancel (the rectangles of those drawn the frame before, `cancel_rects`): that tab's, over the one under the pointer, and over a window in front (the window's rule waits for `escape_docked_seen`, the docked tabs being drawn first). Only with no widget with the keyboard and the last press in no window and no tab with OK and Cancel does the pointer decide (`tabs::Viewer::body`). Tests, each body over both modes with a test for each: `escape_follows_the_keyboard_then_the_pointer[_as_tabs]` (the keyboard's tab over the hovered one; a field focused without a press; a script's window with the keys, and with its tab pressed last; the pointer's tab with the keyboard in nothing), `escape_on_a_docked_tab_gives_way[_as_tabs]` (the keyboard in the docked tab over a window in front). Broken: the window's press counted as nothing ("the window's, its tab pressed", the reviewer's case), the keyboard's tab not first ("closed", "the keyboard's tab"), the pointer never deciding ("the second Escape the tab's"), the focused widget ignored for the last press ("where the keys are"), the window in front over the keyboard's tab ("the keyboard's tab"). (2) **An Escape that sets changes back says so**: one log line, which the Status Bar shows while the log is closed: "Escape canceled Module Properties: its changes are set back. Edit › Undo brings them back." for the kinds whose Cancel is one command (`blueprint::window_drawn_but`, the escaped Cancel); "Escape canceled Options: its changes are set back." for Options (its draft dropped, nothing to undo); nothing where nothing was changed. Of `Tab::escape_cancels`' nine kinds, one Undo restores what Escape set back for `Blueprint`, `Blueprints`, `Instance`, `Instances`, `AreaProperties`, `AreasProperties` and `ModuleProperties` (their Cancel is one `Apply` of the edits set back, `cancel_window`, a step of the module's Undo); not for `Options` (a draft never applied); `TileProperties` asks before it sets anything back, so Escape says nothing there. Tests: `escape_cancels_a_docked_tab_with_ok_and_cancel[_as_tabs]` (the line, the Status Bar's "Last log line: …", one Ctrl+Z bringing Module Properties' and a waypoint's changes back, nothing said unchanged), `escape_closes_docked_options_but_not_a_docked_model_view[_as_tabs]` (Options' line). Broken: no line ("Opened … sample.mod" last), no Options line. (3) **`mg_uikit::segmented`** is a radio group: named (its new `name` argument, the label beside it: the function came with T10 and no tag has it, so its signature changed rather than a second function added), the chosen choice the group's one stop of Tab (the others clicked but not focusable), Left and Right (Up and Down) going to the choice before or after and choosing it, Home and End to the first and the last (the arrows held from egui's focus moves by a focus lock filter), themed or not. Tests: the kit's `a_segmented_control_is_one_stop_its_arrows_choose_and_it_is_named`, the app's `the_switch_is_a_named_radio_group_the_arrows_choose_in`. Broken: the arrows dropped ("ArrowRight moves the focus"), every choice a stop ("the chosen one"), the group unnamed (no radio group "Letters"). (4) **Tests that did not do what they said**: `a_kind_kept_beside_the_middle_opens_in_that_group_as_tabs` and `three_editor_groups_come_back_in_their_order_as_tabs` now open the Faction Editor (no place kept) after the kept ones and assert it lands per the mode (the focused group, or a window); with As Tabs' default a window they fail ("the focused group", "a tab of the focused group or a window"). The Escape tests are a body each with two tests, one per mode, a Properties tab opened by As Tabs' own placement among them (a waypoint's, canceled and undone); the menu's "second Escape" is checked; the waits are for what each looks for (`until`), or a step per input event, said so (`hover`, `click_at`); `screens.rs`'s `shell_open_editors` waits for the tabs, the windows' places and the Properties' page (`frames_until`). (5) `shell_left_folded`'s comment is back on its test. (6) "cancelled" and the other British spellings in the documents T10 touched are American (identifiers in backticks left); the log's entry is under 2026-11-13. (7) **Options pages kept each other's scroll** (the reviewer's `options_pages_whole`: General opened scrolled as far as Area had been, its Editors heading out of sight; the running window the same, one `ScrollArea` for every page): each page has its own (`id_salt` by `OptionsPage`, now `Hash`), opening at its top the first time and where it was left after. Picture before and after (`target/ui-mock/impl/m3-t10/review/`: `general.png`); test `each_options_page_keeps_its_own_scroll` (failed with "General at its top: … -39"). (8) **Left as they are, said here**: a settings file saved by Moonglow 4.1.0 drops `open_editors` (4.1.0 writes only what it knows), so a new installation that runs 4.1.0 once comes back As Floating Windows; nothing in this version can keep it. `Moonglow::moved_by_hand` stays a hidden (`#[doc(hidden)]`) hook for tests that move tabs on the dock itself. **Escape's consumers as they stand, in a frame's order**: `widgets::dialogs_begin` reads it (none while a menu or a popup is open) and `Moonglow::ui` reads where the keyboard is; the Top Bar's menus (a submenu's) and Quick Open (`keep_escape`); the Left Dock (the tree's Filter, `escape_left_search`; the tree's keys); the Bottom Dock (the log handing its arrow keys back); the editor area: each docked tab's body (an area view drops what is in hand with the pointer over it; a docked tab with OK and Cancel notes it where the keyboard is in it, or by the pointer where the keyboard is in nothing; fields read it and the readers that do not consume it, `palette_view`'s prefab rename, `hak_view`'s and `table_view`'s fields, the NUI designer's drags, are kept by the keyboard rule), then each window's (a model window with the pointer over it; the window in front with OK and Cancel, unless a docked tab noted it); after the editor area the docked tab's Escape is kept for the next frame where no window took it and the key is still there; then the dialogs (`escape_closes`, `escape_here`).

**T11. Floating windows and dialogs.**

- **Goal.**
  - Floating editors get the border, 4 px radius and `--shadow`, a bar row (collapse arrow, tabs with ×, Maximize, Close) and a resize grip, keeping `window_bars`.
  - Dialog titles are **left-aligned in a bar row**: `widgets::centered` (widgets.rs:3212) draws its own bar with `title_bar(false)`, with drag by the bar and a Close named "Close". Known difference "window titles centered", settled here.
  - Dialogs open centered in the editor area, so **wizards no longer cover the Welcome subtitle** (known difference, settled).
  - **Remembered window sizes** taken before the theme are scaled once by `Tab::taller()` of their kind (a marker field `window_sizes_scaled`). Known difference, settled.
- **Files.** `lib.rs:2785-2845`, `widgets.rs:3212-3265`, `tabs.rs:100-242` (a `taller_of_kind(&str)`), `settings.rs`.
- **Tests.**
  - Unit: the size migration runs once; a missing marker means unscaled sizes.
  - App: a dialog drags by its bar and closes by its Close; `a_window_is_maximized_by_its_key_and_button_and_stays_so` and `windows_remember_their_size_and_maximize` still pass (migrated where they read sizes).
  - Pictures `shell_floating` against Floating.png; `dialog_windows` and `script_wizard_pages` against Dialogs.png's title rows; `shell_welcome` with the Area Wizard open (the subtitle in sight).
- **Manual.** 02 (window bar paragraph).
- **Risks.** `click_window_close` (common.rs:811) finds egui's close today and must find the new one; about 40 helper uses run through it.
- **Inherits from T9:** a window's tabs have the document tabs' look already (icon, name, dot, close in front and under the pointer); its bar keeps egui_dock's collapse arrow at its left and close all at its right, which the vendored egui_dock keeps for windows alone (`vendor/egui_dock`: the main surface's bars have neither), and `window_bars`' Maximize before the close. `tabs::tab_room` takes a window's bar as 2 × 24 points of those buttons and 28 of Maximize: the bar row T11 builds changes it. No Split Editor on a window's bar (the Floating board).


**Done, 2026-11-13.** **Plan against board** (the boards won where they differ): (1) "Dialogs open centered in the editor area": the Dialogs board says a dialog "opens in the middle of the main window", and the Wizard board puts the Area Wizard at the 1600 by 900 window's middle (`src/Wizard.html`: left 620, top 171, 360 by 557), not the editor area's (its middle 30 points to the left): built as the boards, the window's middle as before, now in one function, `widgets::dialog_center` (centering in the editor area instead would need `Moonglow::editor_area_rect` kept where that function, given the context alone, can read it); with the log open the editor area's middle is higher than the window's, so centering there would move a tall wizard toward the Welcome page's subtitle, not away. The subtitle covered was not reproduced: over the Welcome page only New Module opens (the Area Wizard needs a module, and with one the Welcome page is gone), and it stands below the subtitle (`shell_welcome_wizard`). (2) "A Close named Close" on every dialog: the board puts one "only on a dialog that Esc closes; a window that only its own buttons answer has none" (Unsaved Changes, the Area and Creature Wizards, Delete from Module have none): `widgets::dialog` has it, `widgets::modal` not, but the two pickers the Pickers board draws with one (Select Portrait, Select Texture), whose Close is their Cancel (`Dialog::close_as_cancel`). Named "Close", tipped "Close the dialog (Esc)" (the board's label "Close (Esc)"). (3) The window's Close: the board's aria label is "Close the window and its tabs"; named **Close Window** (**Close Pane** on a pane of a window split in panes), that sentence its tip: a dialog's "Close" and a tab's own Close button (the hak editor's view) can be in sight at once. (4) `--shadow` on windows and dialogs: SPEC rule 7 says "no shadows but under popovers"; the Dialogs and Floating boards draw `--shadow` under both: the theme's `window_shadow` is now the popovers' (6 down, 18 blur). (5) The board's dialog body has 12 points of padding and a 46 point footer with a rule: not built (T11 is the title rows; the body keeps the theme's 8 and the buttons where they are: M4's dialogs). (6) The board's Floating window opens 1040 by 640 at a fixed place; a window still opens at its kind's remembered size (the plan's sizes). (7) The board's collapse arrow is a 22 point button with 3 and 2 points round it and the bar's tools 3 points from the edge; egui_dock's buttons are 24 points at the bar's ends, so the arrow stands a point in from the board's and Close and Maximize two points to the right of it. **Built.** The kit (`mg-uikit`, add-only): `window` module, `title_row` (a dialog's row in a rectangle kept for it: `bar` fill, a `line` under it, the title at the left 12 points in, cut with an ellipsis, a Close icon button at the right where a tip is given, named `CLOSE_NAME`), `title_row_width`, `paint_resize_grip` and `resize_grip_rect` (the board's two strokes in `text-off`, `text-2` hot), `TitleRow`, `metrics::TITLE_ROW` (28); the theme's `window_shadow` (no tag has the kit). `mg-ui`: `widgets::Dialog` (what `modal`, `dialog` and `resizable` make now, with the builder methods the 76 dialogs use): egui's title bar off and the frame's margin moved inside; the row's room kept first and the row painted across the width the body made (in a dialog that grows to what it holds, at least last frame's width and egui's first 320 points and the margin, as egui's title bar kept one: without it a dialog with a grid opened a frame narrower and moved under a click; a dialog resized by its edges is as wide as it is made), the body's drags caught behind it (last frame's body), the grip on a resizable one; `Dialog::close_as_cancel`; `widgets::dialog_center`, `modal_window`, `modal_titled` (an `egui::Modal`'s row, its title named as a label, since a Modal has no name: the job's window, the plugins' questions, a talk-table question, a save a plugin holds). Floating windows: the vendored egui_dock's third addition, `TabViewer::window_bar_button` (`vendor/egui_dock/MOONGLOW.md`), implemented by `tabs::Viewer` (`paint_window_bar_button`: the chevron, Close Window, their names and tips); `Moonglow::window_bars` puts Maximize beside the Close (tip "Fill the main window, or double-click the bar", "Put the window back where it was, or double-click the bar" while maximized) and paints each window's grip; egui's own resize corner is off (`resize_corner_size` 0). `tabs::tab_room` counts a window's bar as `WINDOW_BAR_START` (24) and `WINDOW_BAR_END` (45). **Settings.** `Settings::window_sizes_scaled` (false where a file lacks it), `Settings::scale_window_sizes` (each height by `Tab::taller_of_kind`, a kind with no window or unknown as it was; marked; run by `Moonglow::with_settings` after `shell_migrate`), `new_installation()` marked: a creature's Properties kept 820 by 640 opens 820 by 792, a script's 1120 by 700 opens 1120 by 756; a size past the window is fitted as it opens (`open_window`, as before). 4.1.0 reads this version's file (the field passed over), and a file 4.1.0 writes lacks it, so a round trip through 4.1.0 scales once more (said in the doubts). **Escape and the keys.** A dialog's Close is a click, not in Escape's order: `dialog`'s Escape closes as before (`open_unless_escape`, before it is drawn); a picker's Close is taken as its Cancel the next frame where `cancel_button` takes Escape. Close Window is egui_dock's close all: it asks where a tab asks and "Closing a window cancels its changes" decides, as before; Collapse and Maximize take no key. Tab: a dialog's Close is its last stop (it is laid out after the body); in a window's bar egui_dock's order stands: the tabs, Close Window, Collapse, the body, then Maximize (drawn after the dock); F6 unchanged. **Tests.** `tests/app/dialogs.rs` (new, 12): dragged by its row not its body; Close (in the row, its tip, after the body by Tab) and Escape; no Close on a wizard, its Cancel and Escape; Resize Area by its Close, Escape and Cancel alike, nothing changed; the Area Wizard in the window's middle with each dock open and folded; whole in the 420 point window; a floating window's bar in both modes (Collapse and Expand, Maximize by its button and its key, the grip's strokes and a drag at it, Close Window, the three tips); a size scaled past the window opens inside it; a job's window's row; a picker's Close as its Cancel; a resizable dialog made narrower and wider by its edge. Unit: `window_sizes_from_before_the_theme_are_scaled_once`, `the_marker_is_saved_with_the_sizes_over_another_s_file` (the desktop app's save over another's file), the new installation's third difference, 4.1.0's reading with the field, `Tab::taller_of_kind` in `every_kind_of_tab_has_its_icon`; the kit's `window.rs` (3) and the grip's rect. Migrated: `click_window_close` finds "Close Window" (or "Close Pane") in the bar and fails naming what it found (3 uses, not 40); `click_dialog_s_own_close` for the dialogs with a Close of their own at their foot (Check for Updates, Claims Table, Command Output); `hak_closing`'s menus and `nui.rs`'s Insert… › Close Window (`insert_close_window`) pass over a window's Close Window; the old settings files' sizes scaled once (`resources_panel`, `right_dock`); the theme test's shadow. Each new test was seen to fail with its guard broken (listed in the report). **Pictures** (`target/ui-mock/impl/m3-t11/`, before, dark, light): `shell_floating`, `shell_wizards` (new: `shell_welcome_wizard`, `shell_area_wizard`, `shell_area_wizard_least`), `dialog_windows`, `script_wizard_pages`, `shell_status` (the job), `hak_closing_questions`, `shell_open_editors`. **Inherits to T12 and later:** a dialog's row is `widgets::Dialog` (and `modal_titled` for a Modal), a window's bar buttons `tabs::paint_window_bar_button`: T12's Esc hint applies to a dialog with a Close; T14's yields: Maximize and Close Window take 45 points of a window's bar, and a dialog is at least 338 wide; M4: the dialogs' body padding (12), the footer row and the step list are the board's still to build, and egui_dock's Tab order in a window's bar.

**Review, 2026-11-13.** An independent review of T11 (its commit `fade6611`) found, and this round fixed, each with a test seen to fail first: (1) **every dialog had an unnamed Tab stop**: the drag catcher behind its body was `Sense::drag()`, which in egui 0.36 is focusable; it is `Sense::DRAG` now, and so are the same catchers of a floating window's tab body (`tabs.rs`, "no-window-drag") and the NUI designer's body (`nui_view.rs`), and a window's bar behind Maximize is `Sense::CLICK` (`window_bars`); the grip and the title row take no interaction of their own, the Left Dock's fold buttons and the docks' edges are named buttons (not changed); the talk table's split grip (`talk_view.rs`) is a focusable unnamed handle, older than the branch, left as it was. Tests `every_stop_of_tab_in_a_dialog_is_a_named_control` ("after Shift+Tab 1: a stop with no name") and the floating window's (Tab and Shift+Tab about Maximize: "a stop with no name at … 48 … 76", the bar). (2) **A dialog taller than the window ran off it** (the Area Wizard at 420 by 320 from y −84 to 403): `widgets::Dialog` keeps the size its body took last frame and, where that passes the room under the title row (the window less the row, margin and frame), shows the body in a scroll area of that room, each way; egui then holds the window inside the main window. Tests `a_tall_dialog_stays_whole_and_scrolls_in_a_short_window` (the Area Wizard at 420 by 320 and 800 by 450, Create and Cancel scrolled to and clicked) and `new_module_and_a_picker_stay_whole_in_a_short_window` (New Module; Select Portrait over a creature's Properties) failed with "Select Portrait at [420.0 320.0]: [[-39.0 -79.0] - [459.0 398.0]]" when broken. (3) **A narrow floating window's tabs ran under Maximize and Close Window**: the vendored egui_dock's fourth addition (`DockArea::window_end_room`, `window_min_width`, `vendor/egui_dock/MOONGLOW.md`) leaves `tabs::WINDOW_END_ROOM` (24 points) free of tabs before a window's close all, Maximize stands a point before the close all's button (`WINDOW_BAR_END` 47: where the board has it) and is drawn whatever room the tabs leave, and a window is never narrower than `tabs::window_least_width()`, 211 points (its frame, the arrow, one tab cut to its least with its dot, Maximize and the Close), dragged or given a size. Test `a_window_s_bar_holds_at_every_width` (1, 3 and 8 tabs, 40 to 900 points: each button once and inside the bar, every tab and tab's close before Maximize, no two buttons on one point) failed with the room taken out ("a tab at …, Maximize at …"), with the least width taken out ("Maximize … in …") and with Maximize skipped where the tabs fill the bar ("one Maximize …, []"). (4) **Shift and a window's Close**: egui_dock's own close all, with Shift held, drew a "close window" symbol and did nothing on a click; the painted one closed the window. The painted button now does exactly what egui_dock's does in each mode, and Moonglow turns egui_dock's Shift mode off (`secondary_button_on_modifier(false)`): Shift changes nothing, Close Pane closes its pane's tabs, its menu offers the window's. Test `a_window_s_panes_close_one_at_a_time` (three panes: Close Pane, Shift and Close Pane, a 2DA with changes asking at Close Window) failed with Shift's mode on ("Shift closes the pane alone"). (5) **Dialogs fold again** (the owner's decision): a dialog that egui folded (`collapsible` not set false: Find and Replace Text, Find Instance, Plugins and most of `widgets::dialog`'s) has the arrow at its title row's left, the kit's `title_row_with` (a `fold` option, named "Collapse" and "Expand", tipped "Collapse the dialog to its title row" and "Show the dialog again"), folding it to its row as wide as it was, and back at its size (egui keeps a resized one's); a modal has none. **A difference from the boards**, which draw no arrow on a dialog. Test `a_dialog_folds_to_its_title_row_and_back` (by the pointer and by Enter, the size kept, none on New Module) failed with no arrow ("the arrow at its row's left"). (6) Docs that said what did not run: the Welcome page's covered subtitle reworded above (measured, not settled); the manual's picker with no Close (Select Portrait and Select Texture have one). (7) **Tests that could not fail**: `a_size_scaled_past_the_window_opens_inside_it` asserts the room under the Top Bar ("[[201.0 1.0] - [899.0 799.0]] inside [[0.0 47.0] - …" with `open_window`'s height clamp gone); `click_dialog_s_own_close` takes the one Close under the title row and fails naming what it found. (8) The picker's Close missing a frame: in a window's sizing pass (its first frame, laid out at egui's default place) the title row's buttons take no click now (`ui.is_sizing_pass()`; the kit's `a_title_row_has_no_buttons_in_its_window_s_sizing_pass`), and the test waits for the picker's resting rectangle. (9) The grip is lit where egui takes the corner's drag and no further (`mg_uikit::window::resize_grip_hot`, egui's corner grab radius; the floating window's test, "13 points in: lit false" when broken); `modal_titled`'s titles are title case ("Before Saving", "Not Saved", "This Project Comes with Plugins"); a module's open-tabs record has `rects_scaled` (absent: its windows' heights scaled once on read by their first tab's kind, written with the marker; 4.1.0 passes over it): `windows.rs`'s `an_old_record_s_window_opens_taller_once` (500 made 540, not again, and moved, the script's remembered height 540) failed with the scaling off ("500 made 540: 500") and with the marker not written. **Left as it is, said here**: a resizable dialog's size is egui's persisted memory (`egui::Resize`), not `window_sizes`, and is not scaled by the theme's migration. (10) **Two screens cases the branch broke**: `nui_delete_and_rename` (since T9) found the event script's tab by `label_contains("shop_e.nss")`, which its close button "Close shop_e.nss" matched too: an exact label now (no other such finder in `screens.rs`, `nui.rs` or `tests/app/`); `main_window_log_and_tree` (since T3's review) clicked the area "estate" after Clear Filter, but the tree stays at the tab chosen (a script far down the list) and lays out the rows in sight alone (a new picture, `w3-tree-filter-cleared`, shows the scripts and no areas: the app as designed), so the case scrolls the tree up to the row by the wheel first. Found on the way: the vendored `create_window` set a window's least width after `fixed_size`, which undid a size given (a NUI window put at 1100 opened at 720): the least is set first.
**Merge point M3b, after T11.** Coherent: new installations open editors as tabs and existing ones keep windows; the manual says both.

##### M3c: Widths, Outliner, hints (T12 to T15; merge point after T15)

**T12. Status Bar hints and counts.**

- **Goal.**
  - A hints API (`shell::hints(&[Hint])`, key caps and a word, dropping whole from the end).
  - The **area view's hint line** (area_view.rs:3380-3447) moves into it as key caps, state by state: idle, one selected, several selected, placing a blueprint, placing a light, a brush, scaling, a refused scale, Select Tiles with its notice. Its row in the area view goes.
  - The counts "11 by 11 tiles, 192 objects" move from the area toolbar (3305-3307) to the Status Bar's right.
  - The Welcome page's hints (Ctrl+N, Ctrl+O, Ctrl+P).
- **Files.** `area_view.rs:3300-3447`, `tabs.rs` (welcome), `shell.rs`.
- **Tests.**
  - Unit: hints drop whole at a narrow width.
  - App: each state's hints (migrate the tests reading the hint line; grep the tests for "Click to select" and "Placing").
  - Pictures `shell_main` and `shell_welcome` against Main.png and Welcome.png Status Bars.
- **Manual.** 04-areas (where the hint line is described).
- **Risks.** `frame_perf` run (hints laid out every frame).
- **Inherits from T7:** the Status Bar's middle now holds the log's last line while the Bottom Dock is closed (after a separator beside the switch, `StatusLog`, shown in 48 points or more, cut to the rest): the hints share that middle. The boards cap the line (`.sb-log`'s `max-width`, 96 px on Main, 150 to 260 on others) and give the hints the rest (`flex: 1 1 0`); T12 sets the cap, lays the hints out after it, and keeps `the_status_bar_holds_at_every_width` (status_bar.rs) passing with hints added to its sweep. The counts go before the job item at the right (SPEC: counts, job, module, folder) and yield with the job's name.

**Done, 2026-11-13.** **Plan against board** (the boards won where they differ): (1) The plan's area states list "idle" with the Main-status board's hints Alt + Right Drag Turn and Alt + Drag Raise; the code does neither with nothing selected (Alt + drag moves the view then, Alt + right drag turns nothing): idle shows Click Select, Drag Move or box, Ctrl + Drag Move view, Ctrl + Right Drag Turn view. (2) The plan's "a brush", "a refused scale", "Select Tiles with its notice" and "placing" carried words that are no keys; the boards draw them as a word-only hint at the head (AreaPaint's `.hint.lead` "Painting 2 placeables"): built as `Hint::note`, and a note that does not fit is cut short with an ellipsis (its words in its tip) rather than dropped whole, as a key hint is.
